# Fields 7b-2: Images and File Format 7 Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Add the Image Field (a PNG or JPEG as a field), the `ImageReference` and `NodeSetting.image` settings, the document's `images` table, `GreyImageDecoder`, choosing / linking / relinking / embedding in the inspector, file format 7 with its opening of formats 1 to 6, and lift 7b-1's refusal of a `field` socket on a group's edge.

**Architecture:** A picture is an immutable `GreyImage` (16-bit grey, flat array) held by a new `Field.image` leaf that `FieldSampler` reads bilinearly like any other field. The node's `image` setting is a `ConstantValue.image(ImageReference)`: `.embedded(ImageHash)` (bytes in the document's `images` table, keyed by SHA-256) or `.linked(relativePath:absolutePath:)`. Evaluation gets the embedded bytes and the document's folder through an `ImageResolver` value, and the evaluator mixes what a reference *points at now* (an `ImageStamp`: hash, or a file's path, modification date and size) into the node's cache key, which is how a file edited on disk is noticed at the next evaluation without a file watcher. Pictures are untrusted input, so every limit (16 MB, 8,192 px, PNG and JPEG, regular files only, bounded reads, banded decoding, cancellation) is checked before pixels are decoded, and every failure is an `ImageProblem` sentence on the node, never a crash. The file panels belong to the app shell, so the inspector's image buttons are `InspectorAction`s carried out by `AppModel`.

**Tech Stack:** Swift 6.4 strict concurrency, Swift Testing, ImageIO / CoreGraphics (one file), CryptoKit (SHA-256), Synchronization (`Mutex`), MetalUI file dialogs through the existing `FilePicker`. No new package dependency, no UIKit.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-fields-design.md` §5 (images and file format 7), §3.1 (Image Field), §4 (errors, undo names), §8 (testing), §9 (this plan is the 7b-2 row) and every Errata (7b-1) bullet, notably lifting `GraphContent`'s refusal of a `.field` interface socket (`GroupRefusal.fieldSocket`) in format 7. Parent spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-metalcreator-vertical-slice-design.md` with all its Errata.

**Branch and base:** worktree `/Users/maxburger/Developer/MetalCreator-7b-2`, branch `7b-2`, off master `c20210b` (the 7b-core branch, 3,473 tests). Every test count below is "master + N" against that.

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements include these (copied from CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md and the spec):

- Swift 6 language mode with full strict concurrency (`swift-tools-version: 6.4`); data races are compile errors; no `@unchecked Sendable`, no `nonisolated(unsafe)`. Target macOS 26.
- Tests use **Swift Testing** (`import Testing`, `@Test`, `#expect`), never XCTest.
- `@MainActor @Observable` models for shared state; behaviour in models, views thin; one type per file; no force unwraps or force `try`; no GCD (`DispatchQueue`); `Task.sleep(for:)` only; modern Foundation (`URL.appending(path:)`, `URL.temporaryDirectory`, `replacing(_:with:)`); numbers in messages through `FormatStyle` with `Locale.messages`, never `String(format:)`, never `DateFormatter` or `NumberFormatter`.
- Keep OCCT behind `Kernel`. Fields are computed, never saved; only the **image** setting and the `images` table are new saved data.
- "File format 7 is written always; formats 1 to 6 open unchanged; an older MetalCreator refuses a format-7 file with its existing newer-version message" (spec §5). Adding a `ConstantValue` kind, or anything older readers cannot decode, bumps `GraphFile.currentFormatVersion` (CLAUDE.md).
- "Embedded image limits: 16 MB and 8,192 px per side" (D10). "A linked image is reloaded when its modification date changes, checked at each evaluation" (D11). "Nothing is ever written beside a linked image" (spec §5).
- `Sources/CreatorNodes/Fields/GreyImageDecoder.swift` is the only file that imports ImageIO.
- MetalUI gaps go in `docs/metalui-gaps.md` (entry and summary-table row) and are never worked around here. **This plan found none**; two checks (FI-img-1: a panel that lets a non-image through; FI-img-10: a save panel that replaces a file without asking) are written to log one if a real window shows it.
- `swiftlint lint --strict` reports zero violations before every commit (`.swiftlint.yml`: 140-column lines, trailing commas in multi-line literals).
- A full `swift test` passes only if the exit code is 0, no line says "recorded an issue" or "failed after" (Swift Testing uses glyphs, not ✘), and 11 "Test run with" lines appear.
- The Mac is shared: run full `swift test` in the background to a log and wait for it. **Never** `pkill`/`killall` by a broad pattern (`swift-test`, `swift test`, `swift-build`, `xctest`): other sessions run tests here. Kill only your own process by its captured PID (`$!`), or by a pattern containing your own worktree path.
- Never take screenshots. Never edit `Package.swift`'s MetalUI path. Never modify `../MetalUI`.

## Review Focus

Inputs and conditions the spec implies but only some task's tests exercise; each line names the test that pins it.

1. **A cut-short or damaged picture.** ImageIO reads a truncated PNG as an all-black picture *without an error*, which would silently set every hole to Ø3. A PNG must reach `IEND` (`PNGStructure.isWhole`, Task 3: `PNGStructureTests`, `aPictureCutShortOrWithADamagedBodyIsRefused`), a cut-short JPEG is caught by the source status, noise bytes / empty data / text / PDF give "That file isn't a readable PNG or JPEG image, or it is damaged." and never a crash. A damaged file that is unchanged is not read and decoded again at every evaluation: its failure is remembered under its stamp and retried when the stamp changes (Task 5: `aDamagedFileIsDecodedOnceAndThenFailsAtOnceUntilItChanges`, `aDamagedEmbeddedPictureIsRememberedByItsHash`; a read error is not remembered: `aFileThatCouldNotBeReadIsTriedAgainBecauseFixingThatChangesNoStamp`).
2. **A picture bigger than the limits, or a header lying about its size.** 8,193 px, 100,000 px, a 2³¹ × 2³¹ IHDR with no body, a file one byte over 16 MB, a legal file padded past 16 MB: all refused from the header or the byte count, before any pixel buffer is allocated (Task 3: `aSideBeyond8192PixelsIsRefusedFromTheHeaderAlone`, `aHeaderClaimingAHugePictureIsRefusedWithoutDecoding`, `moreThanSixteenMegabytesIsRefusedBeforeImageIOSeesIt`). The largest legal picture (8,192²) decodes in 32 MB bands to its 128 MB result (`theLargestAllowedPictureDecodesInBands`, about 400 MB peak for the test process, 9 s in debug on an idle Mac); that test's picture is flat, so the band order is pinned by a picture whose every row differs, banded and unbanded (`severalBandsGiveTheSameRowsAsOneBand`), and the largest colour PNG and JPEG were measured (Task 3: about 3 s and 0.7 GB at worst, release).
3. **A linked path that is not a picture file.** A folder, `/dev/zero`, `/dev/null`, a pipe, a file that grows while it is read, a symbolic link: the path must be a regular file (symlinks followed) and at most 16 MB + 1 byte is ever read, and a path swapped for a pipe between the check and the open is refused without waiting (Task 4: `aFolderOrADeviceIsNotAFileAndIsNeverOpened`, `aFileOverSixteenMegabytesIsRefusedUnreadAndOneAtTheLimitIsRead`, `aSymbolicLinkToAFileIsFollowed`, `aPathSwappedForANamedPipeAfterTheCheckIsRefusedNotWaitedOn`).
4. **A document with a damaged `images` entry.** Bad base64, a key that is not a hash, bytes that do not hash to their key, an entry over 16 MB, or an `images` value of the wrong shape: the entry is dropped (alone: a value that is not a string costs only its own picture) and the document opens; the node says its image is missing (Task 6: `aDamagedEntryIsLeftOutAndTheRestOfTheDocumentOpens`, `aPictureOverSixteenMegabytesInATableIsLeftOut`, `anImagesKeyOfTheWrongShapeIsIgnoredNotFatal`, `anEntryThatIsNotEvenAStringCostsThatPictureAndNoOtherOne`, `savingAfterADamagedEntryWasLeftOutWritesTheFileWithoutIt`).
5. **A linked file that moves with, or without, the document.** The relative path is tried first, the absolute one second; Save As elsewhere makes the relative path again from the new folder; a file that cannot be found keeps both stored paths; after Save As the open document follows at once, so a same-named picture in the new folder cannot take the linked one's place; moving the folder holding document and picture still works while the absolute path goes stale (Task 6: `saveAsElsewhereMakesTheRelativePathFromTheNewFolderAndAMissingFileKeepsItsPaths`; Task 9: `afterSaveAsTheOpenDocumentIgnoresALookAlikePictureInTheNewFolder`, `aDocumentFolderMovedWithItsLinkedPictureStillFindsItThroughTheRelativePath`).

Also pinned, in the same words: colour, transparency, 16-bit grey and JPEG decode deterministically (Task 3: `colourBecomesRec709LuminanceOfTheColour`, `transparencyIsCompositedAgainstBlack`, `aGrey16BitPictureKeepsItsPrecision`); a NaN, infinite or `1e300` coordinate samples as a finite value in 0…1 and never traps (Task 2: `nonFiniteAndHugeCoordinatesNeverTrapAndStayInRange`); an image inside a group definition runs with the document's pool and folder, a file edited on disk reaches it, its pictures are kept on save and travel on the clipboard (Task 10: `ImageGroupTests`; Task 6: `imagesUsedInsideAGroupDefinitionAreKept`; Task 8: `aGroupsCopyCarriesThePicturesInsideItsDefinition`); a field wire may cross a group's edge (Task 7: `GroupFieldTests`).


## User decisions

Each is product behaviour or saved data the spec leaves open. The plan is written for the **recommended** option; the alternative is a small, named change.

| # | Decision | Options | Recommendation (what this plan builds) |
|---|---|---|---|
| U1 | **Shape of the `images` table** (file format; the setting's own JSON is U12) | (a) a JSON object `{ "<sha256>": "<base64>" }`, sorted by key; (b) an array of `{ "hash", "data" }` records, sorted by hash (as `definitions` is an array sorted by ID) | **(a).** The spec says "keyed by the SHA-256" and "written sorted by key"; an object makes a duplicate hash impossible. Switching to (b) changes `ImageTable` and two tests. |
| U2 | **A damaged entry in the table** (what opening does) | (a) drop that entry and open the document, the node reports its image missing; (b) refuse the whole file; (c) open and show a banner naming what was dropped | **(a).** One bad picture should not cost the person the document. Entries dropped, each on its own (a neighbour's damage costs nothing, even a value that is not a string): key not 64 lowercase hex digits, value not a string, not base64, over 16 MB, or bytes that do not hash to the key. **In plain terms: a dropped entry is gone from the file for good the next time the person saves**; the file on disk is not changed until then. Keeping the raw entry alive in the session would carry unusable bytes into every later save; (c) needs a notice channel on open that does not exist yet. |
| U3 | **Which version a save writes** (file format) | (a) always 7, as the spec says; (b) 7 only when the document uses an image or a field group socket, else 6 | **(a).** Simple and predictable. **In plain terms: every save by this build writes format 7, including a document with no picture and no field group socket at all, and from then on a 7b-1 (or older) build refuses to open that file with its "saved by a newer version" message.** Opening old files here is unaffected. (b) would be a per-save scan and a surprising version number. |
| U4 | **What a linked image stores** (file format, privacy) | (a) relative path (once the document is saved) **and** absolute fallback, both rewritten at each save from where the file is found; (b) only what was chosen, never rewritten; (c) relative path only once saved | **(a).** It is the spec's design and survives moving the document folder. Note the absolute path puts the person's folder names (often their user name) into a file they may share; (c) avoids that but breaks when only the document moves. |
| U5 | **Embedded bytes are the file's original bytes** (saved data, privacy) | (a) the original PNG/JPEG bytes, EXIF and all (the spec: "the file's original ... bytes"); (b) re-encode without metadata | **(a).** It keeps the hash equal to the file's, keeps "Link Image" lossless, and needs no encoder. Note a phone JPEG can carry GPS in EXIF, which then travels inside shared `.mcgraph` files. |
| U6 | **Colour and transparent pictures** | (a) accept and convert (Rec. 709 luminance of the colour composited against black); (b) refuse non-grey files with a message; (c) accept and warn on the node | **(a).** The spec's decoder describes exactly this conversion, and refusing a colour JPEG is hostile; §10 lists colour *images as a feature* (not as input) out of scope. (c) is a small change (a warning from `evaluate`) once a node can carry one. |
| U7 | **Limits for linked pictures** | (a) the same 16 MB and 8,192 px as embedded ones; (b) no limit for links | **(a).** They bound memory when decoding (the largest legal picture is 128 MB decoded); the spec's D10 only names embedded ones. |
| U8 | **Choose Image embeds by default** | (a) embed; (b) link | **(a).** The document stays portable; "Link Image…" is one click away (D9: chosen per image). |
| U9 | **The Image Field's default edge mode** | (a) Tile (the spec lists it first); (b) Clamp | **(a) Tile**, as listed. Clamp is arguably friendlier for a picture sized to the part (nothing wraps unexpectedly); a one-line change to `ImageFieldNode.inputs` and the tests that read the default's recipe. |
| U10 | **A total size cap for the `images` table** | (a) none beyond 16 MB per picture; (b) a cap (say 64 MB) with a refusal when choosing one more | **(a).** A document of twenty 16 MB pictures is 430 MB of JSON; say so in the risks, add a cap only if it bites. |
| U11 | **A JPEG's EXIF orientation** | (a) ignored: the picture is used as stored; (b) applied | **(b), decided by the user on 2026-10-13 (the plan recommended (a)).** The EXIF/TIFF orientation tag (`kCGImagePropertyOrientation`, 1 to 8) is applied for JPEG and PNG: the grey pixels are the picture as Preview shows it (rotated or flipped; width and height swap for 5 to 8), banded path included. `GreyImage`'s width and height are the oriented ones, and the 8,192 px per side limit applies to the oriented size (identical either way). Human check FI-img-11 confirms a phone photo matches Preview. |
| U12 | **The JSON of the `image` setting** (saved data) | (a) `{"type": "image", "value": {"kind": "embedded", "hash": "<64 hex>"}}` and `{"type": "image", "value": {"kind": "linked", "absolutePath": "/…/vent.png", "relativePath": "maps/vent.png"}}`; `relativePath` is left out until the document has been saved, paths use `/` (macOS only) and a relative one may climb (`../shared/vent.png`); (b) one string with a scheme (`"embedded:<hash>"`, `"file:…"`); (c) the link paths in a separate document-level table | **(a).** It is the existing `{type, value}` shape of every `ConstantValue` with a self-describing `kind`, extensible without another format bump. It is saved data that cannot be changed after release, so confirm it together with U1. Pinned by `ImageReferenceTests.referencesRoundTripAndTheJSONIsPinned`. |
| U13 | **The Image Field's defaults** | (a) 100 × 100 mm, `width` and `height` independent (sliders 0.1 to 1000 mm), so the picture is stretched to the rectangle and **its aspect ratio is not kept**; (b) height follows the picture's aspect with a "keep aspect" switch; (c) a default size from the pixel count | **(a).** The spec lists `width` and `height` as two numbers; wiring either from a measurement stays simple. (b) is a later addition if people square-stretch pictures by accident. |
| U14 | **When an edited file is picked up** | (a) at the next evaluation only (D11: "checked at each evaluation"); (b) also when the window comes forward; (c) a file watcher | **(a).** No watcher and no new hook. **It may surprise a person who edits the picture in another app**: nothing changes in MetalCreator until something re-evaluates (any edit does); FI-img-4 checks exactly that. (b) is a small change if the shell can see window activation (if MetalUI cannot say, that is a gap to log). |
| U15 | **Which image buttons each state offers** | (a) no picture: Choose Image…, Link to File…; embedded: Choose Image… (embeds another), Link Image… (saves the embedded bytes to a file you name, then links it); linked: Relink… (another existing file, linked), Embed Image; (b) always all four plus a "Link to existing file…" | **(a).** Two buttons per state keeps the row short. The gaps it leaves: from *embedded* there is no direct "link an existing different file" (Choose, then Link Image… saves a copy), and from *linked* no direct "choose and embed another" (Relink…, then Embed Image). |
| U16 | **How colour becomes grey** | (a) Rec. 709 luma weights (0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B) on the gamma-encoded sRGB values, alpha composited against black; (b) the same weights on linear light (physically correct brightness; mid greys come out darker: sRGB 50 % reads about 21 %); (c) Rec. 601 weights | **(a).** It is what most image tools mean by "greyscale", so the field matches what the person sees when they convert the picture themselves. |


## File Structure

One responsibility per file, as the codebase does. Where the files live:

| Module | New files | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| `CreatorGraph/Images` | `ImageHash`, `ImageReference` (+`+Codable`), `ImageLimits` | the saved vocabulary: a SHA-256 key, the embedded / linked reference, the 16 MB and 8,192 px limits |
| | `GreyImage`, `ImageEdges` | the decoded picture as plain data and its bilinear, tiled or clamped read |
| | `ImageProblem` | every way a picture is unusable, each with its sentence |
| | `ImageStamp`, `ImageResolver` | what a reference points at now (the cache key) and the bounded reading of its bytes, relative / absolute resolution, rewriting paths on save |
| | `ImageTable`, `Graph+Images` | the `images` table on the way to and from the file; scanning and rewriting a graph's image settings |
| `CreatorGraph/Field` | `Field+OwnPlane` | the plane of the first gradient or image in a field (the preview's slice plane starts there) |
| `CreatorNodes/Fields` | `GreyImageDecoder`, `PNGStructure` | the only ImageIO file; the PNG chunk walk ImageIO cannot be trusted to do |
| | `GreyImageCache`, `ImageLoader` | decoded pictures, and pictures that failed to decode, kept by stamp; the one path from reference to picture |
| `CreatorNodes/Fields/Sources` | `ImageFieldNode` | the node |
| `CreatorApp` | `AppModel+Images`, `ContentType+Images` | the four image buttons over the file panels; the PNG / JPEG panel filters |
| Tests | `Images/*` (Graph), `Fields/*` (Nodes), `ImageInspectorTests` (Editor), `ImageActionTests` (App), `FieldOwnPlaneTests` (Graph), fixtures `TestImages`, `TempFolder`, `TempFolderForNodes`, `ImageProbeNode`, `AppTestImages`, `AppTempFolder` | |

Modified files, by reason: **the setting kind** (`ConstantValue`, `NodeSetting` with its new `reservedForSockets`, read by `GroupNaming` and `SketchSockets`, `Scalar`, `GraphParameterNode`); **the field leaf** (`Field`, `Field+Structure`, `Field+Description`, `FieldSampler`); **evaluation** (`EvalContext`, `EvaluationSetup`, `Evaluator`, `Evaluator+Running`, `DocumentModel`); **the file** (`GraphFile`, plus nine tests that pinned format 6); **groups** (`GraphContent+Apply`, `GroupRefusal`); **the editor** (`InspectorControl`, `InspectorAction`, `UndoName`, `InspectorBuilder`, `InspectorControl+Socket`, `EditorModel+Inspector`, `EditorModel+Editing`, `NodeClipboard`); **the app** (`AppModel`, `AppModel+Files`, `AppModel+Picking`, `DocumentParts`); **the catalog** (`BuiltInNodes`, `OutputNodeTests`, `FieldsLibraryTests`, `CatalogTests`); **docs** (`CLAUDE.md`, the spec's Errata, `human-checks.md`).


## Conventions for every task

- Work in `/Users/maxburger/Developer/MetalCreator-7b-2` only. Build with `swift build --build-tests`, run a subset with `swift test --filter '<regex>'`.
- **Full run** (needed at the end of every task): run it in the background, log to a file, wait, then apply the pass rule. Substitute your session scratch directory for `$SCRATCH`:

```bash
LOG="$SCRATCH/full-task.log"
swift test > "$LOG" 2>&1 &
PID=$!                      # your own process; never pkill by name
wait $PID; echo "exit=$?"
runs=$(grep -c 'Test run with' "$LOG"); bad=$(grep -cE 'recorded an issue|failed after' "$LOG")
total=$(grep 'Test run with' "$LOG" | sed -E 's/.*with ([0-9]+) tests.*/\1/' | paste -sd+ - | bc)
echo "runs=$runs bad=$bad total=$total"   # pass: exit=0, runs=11, bad=0, total as the task says
```

- **Lint** before every commit: `swiftlint lint --strict` must exit 0 with no output under `--quiet`.
- **No new warnings:** after the build, `swift build --build-tests 2>&1 | grep -E '\.swift:[0-9]+:[0-9]+: (warning|error)'` prints nothing for the files you touched (the linker's "built for newer version" lines are expected and not source warnings).
- Commit messages end with the attribution lines your session requires. The commit step names only the files of the task.


### Task 1: The image reference and the `image` setting

The types that reach the file: a content hash, an `ImageReference` (embedded or linked), the `ConstantValue.image` setting kind and `NodeSetting.image`. Nothing evaluates yet. `ImageLimits` (D10) lands here because every later task reads it.

**Files:**

- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/ConstantValue.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageHash.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageLimits.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageReference+Codable.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageReference.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Groups/GroupNaming.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/NodeSetting.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Scalar.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Sketch/SketchSockets.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Values/GraphParameterNode.swift`

Tests:

- Create: `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Images/ImageReferenceTests.swift`
- Modify: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/SketchExposedNameTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `ConstantValue` (a `Hashable`, `Codable` enum with an exhaustive `Kind`), `NodeSetting.all`, `Scalar.init?(_ constant:)`.
- Produces: `ImageHash` (`init(of: Data)`, `init?(hex:)`, `.hex`, `.short`, `Codable`, `Comparable`); `ImageReference` (`.embedded(ImageHash)`, `.linked(relativePath: String?, absolutePath: String)`, `.isEmbedded`, `.hash`, `.fileName`, `Codable`); `ConstantValue.image(ImageReference)`; `NodeSetting.image` (`"image"`, in `NodeSetting.all` but not in `NodeSetting.reservedForSockets`: see the note); `ImageLimits.maxBytes` (16 MiB) and `.maxSide` (8,192).

**Note:** `ConstantValue` gaining a case is the format-affecting change CLAUDE.md talks about ("Adding a `ConstantValue` kind ... bumps the format"); the bump itself is Task 6, where the file learns to write the `images` table. Nothing writes an `image` setting before then. The only exhaustive switch the compiler found outside `ConstantValue.swift` is `GraphParameterNode.swift` and `Scalar.swift`.

**The name `image` stays free for sockets.** Group sockets and exposed Sketch dimensions may not take a setting's name, because those nodes keep their settings beside their inputs' values (`GroupNaming.isReserved`, `SketchSockets.isReserved`, both read `NodeSetting.all` on master). Only Image Field has an `image` setting, so reserving the name would protect nothing and would break files that already exist: a Sketch with an exposed dimension called "image" would silently lose that socket (and the wire into it), and a group with a socket called "image" could no longer be edited. So `NodeSetting.all` still lists every setting (the catalog tests read it that way) and a new `NodeSetting.reservedForSockets` is `all` without `image`; the two reservation checks read it. Two tests pin that such a sketch and such a group still work (`aGroupSocketCalledImageIsStillAllowedAndSurvivesTheFile`, `anExposedDimensionCalledImageIsStillAnInputAndEvaluatesAsBefore`). A later node that keeps an `image` setting *and* exposes sockets would have to reserve it then.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Images/ImageReferenceTests.swift` (new)

```swift
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGraph

/// The image types that reach the file (fields spec §5): the content hash, the reference and its stored setting.
struct ImageReferenceTests {
    static let abc = "ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad"
    static let empty = "e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855"

    @Test func aHashIsTheSHA256OfTheBytesInLowercaseHex() {
        #expect(ImageHash(of: Data("abc".utf8)).hex == Self.abc)
        #expect(ImageHash(of: Data()).hex == Self.empty)
        #expect(ImageHash(of: Data("abc".utf8)).short == "ba7816bf")
    }

    @Test func aHashReadsBackOnlyFromExactlySixtyFourLowercaseHexDigits() {
        #expect(ImageHash(hex: Self.abc) == ImageHash(of: Data("abc".utf8)))
        #expect(ImageHash(hex: Self.abc.uppercased()) == nil, "one spelling only, so equal hashes are equal keys")
        #expect(ImageHash(hex: String(Self.abc.dropLast())) == nil)
        #expect(ImageHash(hex: Self.abc + "0") == nil)
        #expect(ImageHash(hex: String(Self.abc.dropLast()) + "g") == nil)
        #expect(ImageHash(hex: "") == nil)
    }

    @Test func aDamagedHashInAFileFailsToDecode() throws {
        let good = try JSONDecoder().decode(ImageHash.self, from: Data("\"\(Self.abc)\"".utf8))
        #expect(good.hex == Self.abc)
        #expect(throws: DecodingError.self) { try JSONDecoder().decode(ImageHash.self, from: Data("\"nope\"".utf8)) }
    }

    @Test func hashesSortByTheirDigits() {
        #expect([ImageHash(hex: Self.empty), ImageHash(hex: Self.abc)].compactMap { $0 }.sorted().map(\.hex) == [Self.abc, Self.empty])
    }

    @Test func referencesRoundTripAndTheJSONIsPinned() throws {
        let hash = try #require(ImageHash(hex: Self.abc))
        let encoder = JSONEncoder()
        encoder.outputFormatting = .sortedKeys
        let cases: [(ImageReference, String)] = [
            (.embedded(hash), #"{"hash":"\#(Self.abc)","kind":"embedded"}"#),
            (.linked(relativePath: "maps/vent.png", absolutePath: "/a/maps/vent.png"),
             #"{"absolutePath":"\/a\/maps\/vent.png","kind":"linked","relativePath":"maps\/vent.png"}"#),
            (.linked(relativePath: nil, absolutePath: "/a/vent.png"), #"{"absolutePath":"\/a\/vent.png","kind":"linked"}"#),
        ]
        for (reference, json) in cases {
            let data = try encoder.encode(reference)
            #expect(String(bytes: data, encoding: .utf8) == json)
            #expect(try JSONDecoder().decode(ImageReference.self, from: data) == reference)
        }
    }

    @Test func aReferenceWithAnUnknownKindOrNoPathFailsToDecode() {
        for json in [#"{"kind":"cloud"}"#, #"{"kind":"linked"}"#, #"{"kind":"embedded"}"#, #"{"kind":"embedded","hash":"x"}"#] {
            #expect(throws: DecodingError.self) { try JSONDecoder().decode(ImageReference.self, from: Data(json.utf8)) }
        }
    }

    @Test func aLinkedFileIsNamedByItsLastPathComponentAndAnEmbeddedOneHasNoName() throws {
        let vent = ImageReference.linked(relativePath: "maps/vent-mask.png", absolutePath: "/x/maps/vent-mask.png")
        #expect(vent.fileName == "vent-mask.png")
        #expect(ImageReference.linked(relativePath: nil, absolutePath: "/x/y.jpg").fileName == "y.jpg")
        #expect(ImageReference.embedded(try #require(ImageHash(hex: Self.abc))).fileName == nil)
    }

    @Test func theImageSettingIsStoredAsAConstantNoSocketCanHold() throws {
        let reference = ImageReference.linked(relativePath: nil, absolutePath: "/a/b.png")
        let value = ConstantValue.image(reference)
        #expect(NodeSetting.image == "image" && NodeSetting.all.contains(NodeSetting.image))
        #expect(value.isFinite)
        #expect(Scalar(value) == nil, "a setting has no runtime value")
        #expect(try JSONDecoder().decode(ConstantValue.self, from: JSONEncoder().encode(value)) == value)
    }

    /// A file made before 7b-2 may have a group socket called "image"; the new setting must not take the name from it.
    @Test func aGroupSocketCalledImageIsStillAllowedAndSurvivesTheFile() throws {
        #expect(!GroupNaming.isReserved(NodeSetting.image) && !NodeSetting.reservedForSockets.contains(NodeSetting.image))
        #expect(GroupNaming.uniqueSocketName("image", among: []) == "image")
        let sockets = [SocketSpec("image", .number)]
        #expect(GroupNaming.problem(with: sockets) == nil)
        let definition = GroupDefinition(name: "Vent", inputs: sockets)
        let data = try GraphFileIO.encode(GraphFile(definitions: [definition.id: definition]))
        let reopened = try GraphFileIO.decode(data, registry: NodeRegistry([]))
        #expect(reopened.definitions[definition.id]?.inputs.map(\.name) == ["image"])
    }

    @Test func setInputStoresAnImageAndUndoPutsTheOldOneBack() throws {
        let node = Node(typeID: "creator.imageField", name: "Image Field")
        var graph = Graph(nodes: [node.id: node])
        let registry = NodeRegistry([])
        let first = ConstantValue.image(.linked(relativePath: nil, absolutePath: "/a.png"))
        let inverse = try graph.apply(.setInput(node.id, NodeSetting.image, first), registry: registry)
        #expect(graph.nodes[node.id]?.inputValues[NodeSetting.image] == first)
        _ = try graph.apply(inverse, registry: registry)
        #expect(graph.nodes[node.id]?.inputValues[NodeSetting.image] == nil)
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageHash.swift` (new file)

```swift
import CryptoKit
import Foundation

/// The SHA-256 of an image file's bytes, as 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits: the key of the document's `images` table
/// (fields spec §5), so two nodes that use one picture store it once. It is a content address, never a secret.
public struct ImageHash: Hashable, Sendable, Comparable, Codable, CustomStringConvertible {
    /// The 64 digits.
    public let hex: String

    /// The hash of `data`.
    public init(of data: Data) {
        hex = SHA256.hash(data: data).map { byte in
            let digits = String(byte, radix: 16)
            return byte < 16 ? "0" + digits : digits
        }.joined()
    }

    /// The hash written as `hex`, or `nil` unless it is exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits (anything else in a file
    /// is damage, not a hash).
    public init?(hex: String) {
        guard hex.utf8.count == 64, hex.utf8.allSatisfy({ ($0 >= 48 && $0 <= 57) || ($0 >= 97 && $0 <= 102) }) else { return nil }
        self.hex = hex
    }

    /// The first eight digits, enough to tell two embedded images apart in the inspector.
    public var short: String { String(hex.prefix(8)) }

    public var description: String { hex }

    public static func < (lhs: ImageHash, rhs: ImageHash) -> Bool { lhs.hex < rhs.hex }

    public init(from decoder: any Decoder) throws {
        let text = try decoder.singleValueContainer().decode(String.self)
        guard let hash = ImageHash(hex: text) else {
            throw DecodingError.dataCorrupted(.init(codingPath: decoder.codingPath, debugDescription: "Not an image hash."))
        }
        self = hash
    }

    public func encode(to encoder: any Encoder) throws {
        var container = encoder.singleValueContainer()
        try container.encode(hex)
    }
}
```

```swift
/// What an image may be (fields spec D10). Images are untrusted input: a file is checked against these before anything is
/// decoded, so a hostile or mistaken file costs a message, not the machine's memory.
public enum ImageLimits {
    /// The most bytes a PNG or JPEG file may have: 16 MB.
    public static let maxBytes = 16 * 1024 * 1024
    /// The most pixels either side may have.
    public static let maxSide = 8192
}
```

`Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageReference+Codable.swift` (new file)

```swift
extension ImageReference: Codable {
    private enum CodingKeys: String, CodingKey { case kind, hash, relativePath, absolutePath }
    private enum Kind: String, Codable { case embedded, linked }

    public init(from decoder: any Decoder) throws {
        let container = try decoder.container(keyedBy: CodingKeys.self)
        switch try container.decode(Kind.self, forKey: .kind) {
        case .embedded:
            self = .embedded(try container.decode(ImageHash.self, forKey: .hash))
        case .linked:
            self = .linked(relativePath: try container.decodeIfPresent(String.self, forKey: .relativePath),
                           absolutePath: try container.decode(String.self, forKey: .absolutePath))
        }
    }

    public func encode(to encoder: any Encoder) throws {
        var container = encoder.container(keyedBy: CodingKeys.self)
        switch self {
        case .embedded(let hash):
            try container.encode(Kind.embedded, forKey: .kind)
            try container.encode(hash, forKey: .hash)
        case .linked(let relativePath, let absolutePath):
            try container.encode(Kind.linked, forKey: .kind)
            try container.encodeIfPresent(relativePath, forKey: .relativePath)
            try container.encode(absolutePath, forKey: .absolutePath)
        }
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageReference.swift` (new file)

```swift
/// Where an Image Field's picture lives (fields spec §5), stored as the node's `NodeSetting.image`.
public enum ImageReference: Hashable, Sendable {
    /// The file's own PNG or JPEG bytes, stored in the document's `images` table under their hash.
    case embedded(ImageHash)
    /// A file beside the document or elsewhere. `relativePath` is relative to the document's folder and is `nil` until the
    /// document has been saved; `absolutePath` is the fallback when the relative one finds nothing.
    case linked(relativePath: String?, absolutePath: String)

    public var isEmbedded: Bool {
        if case .embedded = self { true } else { false }
    }

    /// The hash of an embedded image.
    public var hash: ImageHash? {
        if case .embedded(let hash) = self { hash } else { nil }
    }

    /// The name of a linked file ("vent-mask.png"); `nil` for an embedded image, which has none.
    public var fileName: String? {
        guard case .linked(let relative, let absolute) = self else { return nil }
        let name = (relative ?? absolute).split(separator: "/").last.map(String.init)
        return name?.isEmpty == false ? name : absolute
    }
}
```

### Task 2: `GreyImage` and the `.image` field

The decoded picture as plain data with its bilinear read, and the new `Field.image` leaf that `FieldSampler` evaluates like any other field. The sampler's single `switch` has no `default`, so the compiler lists the places (`Field.children`, `Field.description`, `Sampling.values`); `Field.ownPlane` is written the same way, and exists so that the preview track (7b-3, `../MetalCreator-7b-3`) does not have to switch over `Field` to find a field's own plane (spec §6).

**Files:**

- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+Description.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+OwnPlane.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+Structure.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/GreyImage.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageEdges.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Fields/FieldSampler.swift`

Tests:

- Create: `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Fields/FieldOwnPlaneTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Images/GreyImageTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Fields/ImageFieldSamplingTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `Field` (`indirect enum`), `FieldSampler.map`, `Plane.yAxis`, `Vector3.normalized` / `.dot`.
- Produces: `ImageEdges` (`.tile = 0`, `.clamp = 1`, `.options`, `.name`); `GreyImage` (`init?(width:height:pixels:)`, `.byteCount`, `value(u:v:edges:)`); `Field.image(GreyImage, plane: Plane, width: Double, height: Double, edges: ImageEdges)`; its recipe `image(4 × 2 px over 8 × 4 mm, tile)`; `estimatedBytes` adds the pixels' bytes; `Field.ownPlane` (the plane of the first linear gradient or image in an expression, depth first from the left; `nil` for the other sources), which 7b-3's slice plane starts on so that it needs no switch over `Field` of its own.

**Note:** `GreyImage.value` folds a position into the picture **while it is still a `Double`** and only then converts to an index, so `1e300`, `-1e300` and `.greatestFiniteMagnitude` cannot trap in `Int(_:)`; NaN and infinities return 0. Row 0 is the top row and the plane's +y is up: `v = 0.5 - t / height`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Images/GreyImageTests.swift` (new)

```swift
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGraph

/// `GreyImage` (fields spec §5): bilinear reads, tiling and clamping, and the inputs a hostile or careless caller can give.
struct GreyImageTests {
    /// Two columns, two rows: black and white on the top row, white and black below.
    static let checker = GreyImage(width: 2, height: 2, pixels: [0, 65_535, 65_535, 0])

    @Test func anImageNeedsPositiveSidesAndExactlyAsManyValuesAsPixels() {
        #expect(GreyImage(width: 0, height: 2, pixels: []) == nil)
        #expect(GreyImage(width: 2, height: -1, pixels: []) == nil)
        #expect(GreyImage(width: 2, height: 2, pixels: [1, 2, 3]) == nil)
        #expect(GreyImage(width: Int.max, height: 2, pixels: [1, 2]) == nil, "the product overflows")
        #expect(Self.checker?.byteCount == 8)
    }

    @Test func pixelCentresReadBackExactlyAndTheMiddleIsTheMean() throws {
        let image = try #require(Self.checker)
        #expect(image.value(u: 0.25, v: 0.25, edges: .clamp) == 0)
        #expect(image.value(u: 0.75, v: 0.25, edges: .clamp) == 1)
        #expect(image.value(u: 0.25, v: 0.75, edges: .clamp) == 1)
        #expect(image.value(u: 0.75, v: 0.75, edges: .clamp) == 0)
        #expect(image.value(u: 0.5, v: 0.5, edges: .clamp) == 0.5)
        #expect(image.value(u: 0.5, v: 0.25, edges: .clamp) == 0.5, "halfway along the top row")
    }

    @Test func clampHoldsTheEdgePixelsOutsideThePicture() throws {
        let image = try #require(Self.checker)
        let cases = [
            (-3.0, 0.25, 0.0), (0.0, 0.25, 0.0), (1.0, 0.25, 1.0), (40.0, 0.25, 1.0), (0.75, -9.0, 1.0),
            (0.75, 9.0, 0.0), (-1e300, -1e300, 0.0), (1e300, 1e300, 0.0),
        ]
        for (u, v, expected) in cases {
            #expect(image.value(u: u, v: v, edges: .clamp) == expected, "u \(u), v \(v)")
        }
    }

    @Test func tileRepeatsThePictureAndBlendsAcrossTheSeam() throws {
        let image = try #require(Self.checker)
        #expect(image.value(u: 1.25, v: 0.25, edges: .tile) == image.value(u: 0.25, v: 0.25, edges: .tile))
        #expect(image.value(u: -0.75, v: 1.75, edges: .tile) == image.value(u: 0.25, v: 0.75, edges: .tile))
        #expect(image.value(u: 1.0, v: 0.25, edges: .tile) == 0.5, "the right edge's white meets the left edge's black")
        #expect(image.value(u: 1.0, v: 0.25, edges: .clamp) == 1, "clamp does not wrap")
    }

    @Test func theBrightestAndDarkestPixelsAreExactlyOneAndZero() throws {
        let image = try #require(GreyImage(width: 1, height: 1, pixels: [65_535]))
        for edges in ImageEdges.allCases {
            #expect(image.value(u: 0.3, v: 0.9, edges: edges) == 1)
            #expect(image.value(u: -7.5, v: 12.5, edges: edges) == 1)
        }
    }

    @Test func nonFiniteAndHugeCoordinatesNeverTrapAndStayInRange() throws {
        let image = try #require(Self.checker)
        let odd = [
            Double.nan, .infinity, -.infinity, 1e300, -1e300, .greatestFiniteMagnitude, -.greatestFiniteMagnitude,
            .leastNonzeroMagnitude,
        ]
        for edges in ImageEdges.allCases {
            for u in odd {
                for v in odd {
                    let value = image.value(u: u, v: v, edges: edges)
                    #expect((0.0...1.0).contains(value), "u \(u), v \(v), \(edges)")
                    if !u.isFinite || !v.isFinite { #expect(value == 0) }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    @Test func theEdgeModesHaveTheirSegmentedControlOrder() {
        #expect(ImageEdges.options == ["Tile", "Clamp"])
        #expect(ImageEdges(rawValue: 0) == .tile && ImageEdges(rawValue: 1) == .clamp && ImageEdges(rawValue: 2) == nil)
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import Testing
@testable import CreatorNodes

/// The `.image` field through `FieldSampler` (fields spec §5): the projection, the edge modes, the recipe and the size.
struct ImageFieldSamplingTests {
    /// 4 × 2 px: the top row ramps 0, 1/3, 2/3, 1 and the bottom row is its mirror.
    static let ramp = GreyImage(width: 4, height: 2, pixels: [0, 21_845, 43_690, 65_535, 65_535, 43_690, 21_845, 0])

    func field(plane: Plane = .xy, width: Double = 8, height: Double = 4, edges: ImageEdges = .clamp) throws -> Field {
        .image(try #require(Self.ramp), plane: plane, width: width, height: height, edges: edges)
    }

    @Test func aPointMapsToTheImageCentredOnThePlaneWithRowZeroAtTheTop() async throws {
        let field = try field()
        // 8 × 4 mm centred on the origin: x −3 is the centre of column 0, y +1 the centre of row 0.
        let found = try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: [Vector3(-3, 1, 0), Vector3(3, 1, 0), Vector3(-3, -1, 0), Vector3(3, -1, 0)],
                                                  kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(found == [0, 1, 1, 0])
    }

    @Test func theDistanceAlongTheNormalDoesNotMatterBecauseTheImageIsProjected() async throws {
        let found = try await FieldSampler.sample(try field(), at: [Vector3(1, 1, 0), Vector3(1, 1, 50), Vector3(1, 1, -50)],
                                                  kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(found[0] == found[1] && found[1] == found[2])
    }

    @Test func aTiltedPlaneProjectsAlongItsOwnNormal() async throws {
        // The front plane: local x is world x, local y is world z, and the normal is −y.
        let field = try field(plane: .xz)
        let points = [Vector3(-3, 7, 1), Vector3(3, -7, 1), Vector3(3, 0, -1)]
        let found = try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: points, kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(found == [0, 1, 0])
    }

    @Test func aMovedPlaneMovesTheImage() async throws {
        let moved = Plane(origin: Vector3(100, 0, 0), normal: .unitZ, xAxis: .unitX)
        let found = try await FieldSampler.sample(try field(plane: moved), at: [Vector3(97, 1, 0), Vector3(-3, 1, 0)], kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(found == [0, 0], "the second point is 103 mm left of the image; clamp holds its edge")
    }

    @Test func clampHoldsTheEdgeAndTileRepeats() async throws {
        let far = [Vector3(-3 + 8, 1, 0), Vector3(-3 - 8, 1, 0), Vector3(1000, 1, 0)]
        let clamped = try await FieldSampler.sample(try field(edges: .clamp), at: far, kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(clamped == [1, 0, 1])
        let tiled = try await FieldSampler.sample(try field(edges: .tile), at: far, kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(tiled[0] == 0 && tiled[1] == 0, "one whole width away is the same pixel")
        #expect((0.0...1.0).contains(tiled[2]))
    }

    @Test func aBatchAgreesExactlyWithOnePointAtATime() async throws {
        let field = try field(edges: .tile)
        let points = (0..<50).map { Vector3(Double($0) * 0.73 - 12, Double($0 % 7) - 3.1, Double($0)) }
        let batch = try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: points, kernel: FakeKernel())
        for (index, point) in points.enumerated() {
            #expect(try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: [point], kernel: FakeKernel()) == [batch[index]])
        }
    }

    @Test func nonFinitePointsSampleAsZeroAndNeverTrap() async throws {
        let points = [Vector3(.nan, 0, 0), Vector3(0, .infinity, 0), Vector3(1e300, -1e300, 0)]
        let found = try await FieldSampler.sample(try field(edges: .tile), at: points, kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(found[0] == 0 && found[1] == 0)
        #expect((0.0...1.0).contains(found[2]))
    }

    @Test func itComposesWithTheMathsNodesLikeAnyField() async throws {
        let scaled = Field.multiply(try field(), .constant(2))
        let found = try await FieldSampler.sample(scaled, at: [Vector3(3, 1, 0)], kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(found == [2])
    }

    @Test func theRecipeNamesTheImageAndItsPlacement() throws {
        #expect(try field(width: 8, height: 4, edges: .tile).description == "image(4 × 2 px over 8 × 4 mm, tile)")
        #expect(try field(width: 1234.5, height: 2, edges: .clamp).description == "image(4 × 2 px over 1,234.5 × 2 mm, clamp)")
    }

    @Test func theFieldCountsItsImagesBytesAndIsAOneNodeLeaf() throws {
        let leaf = try field()
        #expect(leaf.children.isEmpty && leaf.nodeCount == 1)
        #expect(leaf.estimatedBytes == 64 + 32 + 16, "eight 16-bit pixels")
        let sum = Field.add(leaf, .multiply(leaf, .constant(1)))
        #expect(sum.estimatedBytes == 64 + 32 * 5 + 2 * 16)
    }
}
```

```swift
/// `Field.ownPlane`: the plane a gradient or an image lies on, which the preview's slice plane (7b-3) starts on.
struct FieldOwnPlaneTests {
    let tilted = Plane(origin: Vector3(0, 0, 5), normal: .unitX, xAxis: .unitY)

        let image = Field.image(try #require(picture), plane: tilted, width: 4, height: 4, edges: .tile)
        #expect(image.ownPlane == tilted)
    }

        #expect(Field.constant(1).ownPlane == nil)
        #expect(Field.radialGradient(center: .zero, radius: 3).ownPlane == nil)
        #expect(Field.noise(seed: 1, scale: 5, octaves: 2).ownPlane == nil)
        #expect(Field.distance(.point(.zero), signed: false).ownPlane == nil)
    }

        let image = Field.image(try #require(picture), plane: tilted, width: 4, height: 4, edges: .clamp)
        let gradient = Field.linearGradient(plane: .xy, length: 2)
        let noise = Field.noise(seed: 1, scale: 5, octaves: 2)

        #expect(Field.multiply(gradient, image).ownPlane == .xy)
        #expect(Field.multiply(image, gradient).ownPlane == tilted)
        #expect(Field.clamp(.smoothMinimum(noise, .constant(2), width: 1), low: 0, high: 1).ownPlane == nil)
    }
```

`Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/GreyImage.swift` (new file)

```swift
/// A decoded picture as grey values (fields spec §5): `width` × `height` pixels of 16-bit grey, row 0 at the top, in one flat
/// array, so copies are cheap and a field holding one costs `byteCount`. Made by `GreyImageDecoder` (CreatorNodes), which is
/// the only reader of image files; this type is plain data and maths.
public struct GreyImage: Sendable {
    public let width: Int
    public let height: Int
    /// Row-major grey values, 0 black to 65,535 white.
    public let pixels: [UInt16]

    /// `nil` unless both sides are at least 1 and `pixels` has exactly `width * height` values.
    public init?(width: Int, height: Int, pixels: [UInt16]) {
        let (count, overflow) = width.multipliedReportingOverflow(by: height)
        guard width >= 1, height >= 1, !overflow, pixels.count == count else { return nil }
        self.width = width
        self.height = height
        self.pixels = pixels
    }

    /// Memory the pixels take.
    public var byteCount: Int { pixels.count * MemoryLayout<UInt16>.stride }

    /// The grey value in 0…1 at picture coordinates `u` (0 left edge, 1 right edge) and `v` (0 top edge, 1 bottom edge),
    /// bilinear between pixel centres. Outside 0…1 the picture repeats or holds its edge pixels, as `edges` says. A
    /// coordinate that is NaN or infinite gives 0, and no finite one can trap: positions are folded into the picture while
    /// still `Double`s.
    public func value(u: Double, v: Double, edges: ImageEdges) -> Double {
        let x = u * Double(width) - 0.5, y = v * Double(height) - 0.5
        guard x.isFinite, y.isFinite else { return 0 }
        let columns = Self.taps(x, count: width, edges: edges), rows = Self.taps(y, count: height, edges: edges)
        let top = Self.mix(grey(columns.low, rows.low), grey(columns.high, rows.low), columns.fraction)
        let bottom = Self.mix(grey(columns.low, rows.high), grey(columns.high, rows.high), columns.fraction)
        return Self.mix(top, bottom, rows.fraction) / 65_535
    }

    private func grey(_ x: Int, _ y: Int) -> Double { Double(pixels[y * width + x]) }

    private static func mix(_ a: Double, _ b: Double, _ t: Double) -> Double { a + (b - a) * t }

    /// The two pixels along one axis that a position (in pixels, centres at whole numbers) lies between, and how far towards
    /// the second.
    private static func taps(_ position: Double, count: Int, edges: ImageEdges) -> (low: Int, high: Int, fraction: Double) {
        let size = Double(count)
        switch edges {
        case .tile:
            let folded = position - (position / size).rounded(.down) * size
            let held = min(max(folded, 0), size.nextDown)
            let low = Int(held.rounded(.down))
            return (low, (low + 1) % count, held - Double(low))
        case .clamp:
            let held = min(max(position, 0), size - 1)
            let low = Int(held.rounded(.down))
            return (low, min(low + 1, count - 1), held - Double(low))
        }
    }
}
```

```swift
/// What an Image Field does outside its picture's rectangle (fields spec §5). The raw value is the index of the inspector's
/// segmented control, stored in an integer socket.
public enum ImageEdges: Int, Sendable, CaseIterable {
    /// The picture repeats.
    case tile = 0
    /// The nearest edge pixel is held.
    case clamp = 1

    /// The names the segmented control shows, in raw-value order.
    public static let options = ["Tile", "Clamp"]

    /// The edge mode a recipe names ("tile", "clamp").
    public var name: String { self == .tile ? "tile" : "clamp" }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+OwnPlane.swift` (new file)

```swift
import CreatorGeometry

extension Field {
    /// The plane this field is laid out on, if it has one: the plane of the first linear gradient or image in the expression,
    /// searched depth first from the left (fields spec §6 starts the preview's slice plane there). A distance, a radial
    /// gradient, noise or a constant has none, so an expression made only of those has none. No `default`: a new case must say
    /// whether it carries a plane.
    public var ownPlane: Plane? {
        switch self {
        case .linearGradient(let plane, _), .image(_, let plane, _, _, _):
            plane
        case .constant, .distance, .radialGradient, .noise:
            nil
        case .add, .multiply, .minimum, .maximum, .remap, .clamp, .smoothMinimum:
            children.lazy.compactMap(\.ownPlane).first
        }
    }
}
```

### Task 3: `ImageProblem` and `GreyImageDecoder`

Untrusted pictures. `ImageProblem` is every way a picture can be unusable, each with its sentence. `GreyImageDecoder` is the only ImageIO file: `inspect` reads the header and refuses by size, type and structure before any pixel is decoded; `decode` converts in 32 MB bands off the caller's actor. `PNGStructure` exists because ImageIO decodes a cut-short PNG as a black picture and reports success (found by running it: the test `aPictureCutShortOrWithADamagedBodyIsRefused` failed on the first draft).

**Files:**

- Create: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageProblem.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Fields/GreyImageDecoder.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Fields/PNGStructure.swift`

Tests:

- Create: `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Images/ImageProblemTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Fields/GreyImageDecoderTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Fields/PNGStructureTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Support/TestImages.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `ImageLimits` and `GreyImage` (Tasks 1 and 2); `Locale.messages` (CreatorKernel).
- Produces: `ImageProblem` (`.notChosen`, `.tooManyBytes(Int)`, `.tooManyPixels(width:height:)`, `.unsupportedType`, `.undecodable`, `.notAFile(name:)`, `.unreadable(name:reason:)`, `.notFound(name:folder:)`, `.embeddedMissing`; `.message`); `GreyImageDecoder.inspect(_:) throws(ImageProblem) -> Info(width:height:format:)`; `@concurrent GreyImageDecoder.decode(_:) async throws -> GreyImage` (and the internal `decode(_:bandBytes:)` it calls); `GreyImageDecoder.Format` (`.png`, `.jpeg`, `.fileExtension`); `PNGStructure.isWhole(_:)`; test fixtures `TestImages`.

**Note:** Measured here: the largest legal picture (8,192 × 8,192 grey) decodes in about 9 s in a debug build and the whole test process peaks near 400 MB; ImageIO draws monochrome files into a 16-bit gray context (exact: the 16-bit test returns the stored 4,660) and everything else into 16-bit sRGB RGBA, premultiplied, where Rec. 709 luminance of the premultiplied components is the composite against black. The EXIF/TIFF orientation tag is applied (decision U11 (b)), so the grey grid is the picture as Preview shows it. Colour management is the OS's, so a colour picture can differ by a count or two between OS versions: the colour tests allow ±2 of 65,535.

**The largest colour pictures, measured (release build, this Mac).** A standalone program built from this task's `GreyImageDecoder`, `PNGStructure`, `GreyImage` and `ImageProblem` decoded 8,192 × 8,192 pictures small enough for the 16 MB limit (PNGs written with a gradient first row and "up"-filtered zero rows after it, so 67 megapixels deflate to under 1 MB; the JPEG with ImageIO). Three runs each, on a busy machine (load average about 80, so read the times as upper bounds); the footprint is the peak memory footprint from `/usr/bin/time -l`:

| Picture (8,192 × 8,192) | File | Time | Peak footprint |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8-bit grey PNG | 85 KB | 0.2 to 0.35 s | 239 MB |
| 16-bit grey PNG | 167 KB | 0.35 to 0.5 s | 306 MB |
| 8-bit RGB PNG | 217 KB | 2.1 to 3.0 s | 677 MB |
| 16-bit RGBA PNG | 598 KB | 1.4 to 1.8 s | 711 MB |
| colour JPEG | 1.1 MB | 0.65 to 0.8 s | 210 MB |

These numbers changed the code. The first draft drew each 32 MB band from a `CGImage` made with `kCGImageSourceShouldCache: false`, and for a PNG ImageIO then decodes the whole file again for every band: the colour PNGs took 4 to 21 s (52 s with 4 MB bands, 16 to 128 passes), with the same memory. So a PNG is now decoded once up front (`kCGImageSourceShouldCacheImmediately`) and the bands are drawn from that; a JPEG, which ImageIO reads band by band, keeps the lazy path (decoding it up front would add 330 MB). The banding stays because it bounds the *result* (one 128 MB array plus a 32 MB band) and lets a cancelled decode stop between bands. **No cap on decoded bytes by format was added**: the worst legal picture costs about 3 s and 0.7 GB once, off the main actor and cancellable, and the grey result that stays in the cache is 128 MB at most. If that proves too much, the cap is a product limit (say, colour pictures over 40 megapixels refused with a message), which is user decision U7's follow-up, not a quiet change.

**The banding test has to be able to fail.** A flat picture decodes identically however it is banded, so `theLargestAllowedPictureDecodesInBands` cannot catch a band placed a row off. `GreyImageDecoder.decode(_:bandBytes:)` takes the band size (internal, for tests); `severalBandsGiveTheSameRowsAsOneBand` decodes a 37 × 30 picture whose every row differs, grey and colour, in bands of 4, 3 and 1 rows (and a band smaller than one row) and compares the whole result with a single-band decode and the first, middle and last rows with the values written. (Checked by breaking the crop offset: the test fails.)

**The size limit reads "16 MB" everywhere.** `ImageLimits.maxBytes` is 16 MiB; the message writes both numbers in `.byteCount(style: .memory)`, which prints 16 MB (`.file` style would print 16.8 MB, which matches neither the spec, the D10 table nor human check FI-img-2).

`Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Fields/GreyImageDecoderTests.swift` (new)

```swift
import CreatorGraph
import Foundation
import Testing
import UniformTypeIdentifiers
@testable import CreatorNodes

/// `GreyImageDecoder` (fields spec §5, D10): grey and colour pictures, and every way an untrusted file can be wrong.
struct GreyImageDecoderTests {
    @Test func aGrey8BitPictureReadsBackAsItsValuesScaledTo16Bits() async throws {
        let png = TestImages.grey(4, 2) { x, y in x * 60 + y * 10 }
        let image = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(png)
        #expect(image.width == 4 && image.height == 2)
        #expect(image.pixels == [0, 60, 120, 180, 10, 70, 130, 190].map { $0 * 257 }, "row 0 is the top row")
    }

    @Test func aGrey16BitPictureKeepsItsPrecision() async throws {
        let png = TestImages.grey(3, 1, bits: 16) { x, _ in [0, 4_660, 65_535][x] }
        #expect(try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(png).pixels == [0, 4_660, 65_535])
    }

    @Test func colourBecomesRec709LuminanceOfTheColour() async throws {
        let expected: [(Int, Int, Int, Int)] = [
            (255, 0, 0, 13_932), (0, 255, 0, 46_870), (0, 0, 255, 4_732), (255, 255, 255, 65_535), (0, 0, 0, 0),
        ]
        for (red, green, blue, grey) in expected {
            let image = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(TestImages.colour(2, 2, red: red, green: green, blue: blue))
            for pixel in image.pixels { #expect(abs(Int(pixel) - grey) <= 2, "(\(red), \(green), \(blue)) gave \(pixel), not \(grey)") }
        }
    }

    @Test func transparencyIsCompositedAgainstBlack() async throws {
        let clear = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(TestImages.colour(2, 2, red: 255, green: 255, blue: 255, alpha: 0))
        #expect(clear.pixels.allSatisfy { $0 == 0 }, "a see-through white is black")
        let half = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(TestImages.colour(2, 2, red: 255, green: 255, blue: 255, alpha: 128))
        #expect(half.pixels.allSatisfy { abs(Int($0) - 32_896) <= 4 })
    }

    @Test func aJPEGDecodesToItsGreyWithinCompressionError() async throws {
        let jpeg = TestImages.flatGrey(16, 16, 128, type: .jpeg)
        let image = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(jpeg)
        #expect(image.width == 16 && image.pixels.allSatisfy { abs(Int($0) - 128 * 257) <= 600 })
        #expect(try GreyImageDecoder.inspect(jpeg).format == .jpeg)
    }

    @Test func inspectReadsTheHeaderAndNamesTheFormat() throws {
        let info = try GreyImageDecoder.inspect(TestImages.flatGrey(30, 20, 9))
        #expect(info == GreyImageDecoder.Info(width: 30, height: 20, format: .png))
        #expect(info.format.fileExtension == "png" && GreyImageDecoder.Format.jpeg.fileExtension == "jpg")
    }

    // MARK: Damaged, foreign and hostile files

    @Test func bytesThatAreNotAnImageAreRefused() async throws {
        var state: UInt32 = 7
        let noise = Data((0..<1_000).map { _ in state = state &* 1_664_525 &+ 1_013_904_223; return UInt8(state >> 24) })
        for bytes in [Data(), Data("hello, this is a text file".utf8), noise, Data("%PDF-1.4\n%%EOF".utf8)] {
            await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.self) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(bytes) }
            #expect(throws: ImageProblem.self) { try GreyImageDecoder.inspect(bytes) }
        }
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.undecodable) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(Data("hello".utf8)) }
    }

    @Test func aPictureCutShortOrWithADamagedBodyIsRefused() async throws {
        let png = TestImages.noisy(64)
        #expect(png.count > 2_000, "the fixture must have a body to cut")
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.undecodable) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(png.prefix(png.count * 6 / 10)) }
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.undecodable) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(png.dropLast(4)) }
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.self) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(png.prefix(20)) }
        var damaged = png
        for offset in stride(from: 100, to: 400, by: 7) { damaged[offset] ^= 0xFF }
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.undecodable) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(damaged) }
        let jpeg = TestImages.noisy(64, type: .jpeg)
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.undecodable) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(jpeg.prefix(jpeg.count / 2)) }
    }

    @Test func trailingBytesAfterAWholePictureAreIgnored() async throws {
        var png = TestImages.flatGrey(4, 4, 200)
        png.append(Data(repeating: 0, count: 100))
        #expect(try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(png).pixels.allSatisfy { $0 == 200 * 257 })
    }

    @Test func formatsThatAreNotPNGOrJPEGAreRefusedEvenWhenImageIOReadsThem() async throws {
        for type in [UTType.gif, .tiff] {
            let data = TestImages.flatGrey(8, 8, 90, type: type)
            #expect(!data.isEmpty, "\(type) fixture")
            await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.unsupportedType) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(data) }
        }
    }

    @Test func aSideBeyond8192PixelsIsRefusedFromTheHeaderAlone() async throws {
        for (width, height) in [(8_193, 1), (1, 8_193), (100_000, 1)] {
            let data = TestImages.flatGrey(width, height, 5)
            let expected = ImageProblem.tooManyPixels(width: width, height: height)
            #expect(throws: expected) { try GreyImageDecoder.inspect(data) }
            await #expect(throws: expected) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(data) }
        }
        for (width, height) in [(8_192, 1), (1, 8_192)] {
            let image = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(TestImages.flatGrey(width, height, 5))
            #expect(image.width == width && image.height == height)
        }
    }

    @Test func aHeaderClaimingAHugePictureIsRefusedWithoutDecoding() async throws {
        for (width, height) in [(UInt32(0x7FFF_FFFF), UInt32(0x7FFF_FFFF)), (100_000, 100_000), (0, 0)] {
            let bomb = TestImages.pngHeader(claiming: width, height)
            await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.self) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(bomb) }
        }
    }

    @Test func moreThanSixteenMegabytesIsRefusedBeforeImageIOSeesIt() async throws {
        let over = Data(count: ImageLimits.maxBytes + 1)
        #expect(throws: ImageProblem.tooManyBytes(ImageLimits.maxBytes + 1)) { try GreyImageDecoder.inspect(over) }
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.tooManyBytes(ImageLimits.maxBytes + 1)) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(over) }
        var padded = TestImages.flatGrey(4, 4, 1)
        padded.append(Data(count: ImageLimits.maxBytes))
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.self) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(padded) }
        await #expect(throws: ImageProblem.undecodable) { try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(Data(count: ImageLimits.maxBytes)) }
    }

    @Test(.timeLimit(.minutes(2))) func theLargestAllowedPictureDecodesInBands() async throws {
        let big = TestImages.flatGrey(8_192, 8_192, 77)
        #expect(big.count < ImageLimits.maxBytes)
        let image = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(big)
        #expect(image.width == 8_192 && image.height == 8_192 && image.byteCount == 128 * 1_024 * 1_024)
        #expect(image.pixels.first == 77 * 257 && image.pixels.last == 77 * 257 && image.pixels[8_192 * 4_096 + 5] == 77 * 257)
    }

    /// A picture in which no two rows are alike, taken in bands of 1, 3 and 4 rows (and one smaller than a row), must come out
    /// exactly as one taken in a single band: a band placed a row off, in the wrong order or cropped wrongly cannot hide in a
    /// flat picture, but it shows in this one at its first, middle and last rows.
    @Test func severalBandsGiveTheSameRowsAsOneBand() async throws {
        let width = 37, height = 30  // a grey row is 74 bytes of 16-bit bitmap, a colour row 296
        let grey = TestImages.grey(width, height) { x, y in (x * 7 + y * 13) % 256 }
        let whole = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(grey, bandBytes: GreyImageDecoder.defaultBandBytes)
        for y in [0, 1, 14, 15, 28, 29] {
            for x in [0, 1, 20, 36] { #expect(whole.pixels[y * width + x] == UInt16((x * 7 + y * 13) % 256 * 257), "grey (\(x), \(y))") }
        }
        for rows in [4, 3, 1] {
            let banded = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(grey, bandBytes: 74 * rows)
            #expect(banded.pixels == whole.pixels, "grey in bands of \(rows) rows")
        }
        #expect(try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(grey, bandBytes: 1).pixels == whole.pixels, "a band smaller than a row is one row")

        let colour = TestImages.colourRows(width, height)
        let wholeColour = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(colour, bandBytes: GreyImageDecoder.defaultBandBytes)
        for y in [0, 1, 14, 15, 28, 29] {
            let (red, green, blue) = TestImages.pixel(5, y)
            let expected = (2126 * red + 7152 * green + 722 * blue) * 257 / 10_000
            #expect(abs(Int(wholeColour.pixels[y * width + 5]) - expected) <= 4, "colour row \(y)")
        }
        for rows in [4, 3, 1] {
            let banded = try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(colour, bandBytes: 296 * rows)
            #expect(banded.pixels == wholeColour.pixels, "colour in bands of \(rows) rows")
        }
        #expect(Set(wholeColour.pixels[0..<width]) != Set(wholeColour.pixels[(height - 1) * width..<height * width]))
    }

    @Test func aCancelledDecodeStopsWithCancellationNotAnImageProblem() async throws {
        let png = TestImages.flatGrey(64, 64, 3)
        let task = Task {
            while !Task.isCancelled { await Task.yield() }
            return try await GreyImageDecoder.decode(png)
        }
        task.cancel()
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
    }
}
```

```swift
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorNodes

/// `PNGStructure`: ImageIO decodes a PNG cut short as a black picture without complaint, so completeness is checked here.
struct PNGStructureTests {
    @Test func aWholePNGPasses() {
        let png = TestImages.noisy(32)
        #expect(PNGStructure.isWhole(png))
        #expect(PNGStructure.isWhole(png + Data(repeating: 7, count: 50)), "bytes after IEND are ignored")
    }

    @Test func aPNGCutAnywhereBeforeItsEndFails() {
        let png = TestImages.noisy(32)
        for length in [0, 7, 8, 20, 33, png.count / 2, png.count - 13, png.count - 1] {
            #expect(!PNGStructure.isWhole(png.prefix(length)), "cut to \(length) of \(png.count) bytes")
        }
    }

    @Test func notAPNGFailsAndAHugeChunkLengthCannotTrap() {
        #expect(!PNGStructure.isWhole(Data("hello, this is not a picture at all".utf8)))
        var forged = Data([0x89, 0x50, 0x4E, 0x47, 0x0D, 0x0A, 0x1A, 0x0A, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF])
        forged.append(Data("IDAT".utf8))
        forged.append(Data(count: 8))
        #expect(!PNGStructure.isWhole(forged), "a length of 4 GB runs past the data")
    }
}
```

`Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Support/TestImages.swift` (new)

```swift
// Test fixture file: PNG, JPEG, GIF and TIFF bytes made with ImageIO, and the damaged files the image tests feed the decoder.
import CoreGraphics
import Foundation
import ImageIO
import UniformTypeIdentifiers

enum TestImages {
    /// A `width` × `height` monochrome picture of `bits` (8 or 16) per pixel; `value(x, y)` is 0…255 or 0…65,535.
    static func grey(_ width: Int, _ height: Int, bits: Int = 8, type: UTType = .png, _ value: (Int, Int) -> Int) -> Data {
        let order = bits == 16 ? CGBitmapInfo.byteOrder16Little.rawValue : 0
        guard let context = CGContext(data: nil, width: width, height: height, bitsPerComponent: bits,
                                      bytesPerRow: width * bits / 8, space: CGColorSpaceCreateDeviceGray(),
                                      bitmapInfo: CGImageAlphaInfo.none.rawValue | order),
              let memory = context.data else { return Data() }
        if bits == 16 {
            let pixels = memory.bindMemory(to: UInt16.self, capacity: width * height)
            for y in 0..<height { for x in 0..<width { pixels[y * width + x] = UInt16(littleEndian: UInt16(value(x, y))) } }
        } else {
            let pixels = memory.bindMemory(to: UInt8.self, capacity: width * height)
            for y in 0..<height { for x in 0..<width { pixels[y * width + x] = UInt8(value(x, y)) } }
        }
        return encode(context.makeImage(), as: type)
    }

    /// One grey (0…255) over the whole picture, painted with a fill, not pixel by pixel, so a large picture is cheap.
    static func flatGrey(_ width: Int, _ height: Int, _ level: Double, type: UTType = .png) -> Data {
        let space = CGColorSpaceCreateDeviceGray()
        guard let context = CGContext(data: nil, width: width, height: height, bitsPerComponent: 8, bytesPerRow: width,
                                      space: space, bitmapInfo: CGImageAlphaInfo.none.rawValue) else { return Data() }
        context.setFillColor(gray: level / 255, alpha: 1)
        context.fill(CGRect(x: 0, y: 0, width: width, height: height))
        return encode(context.makeImage(), as: type)
    }

    /// A single sRGB colour (components 0…255) with `alpha` (0…255) over a `width` × `height` PNG.
    static func colour(_ width: Int, _ height: Int, red: Int, green: Int, blue: Int, alpha: Int = 255) -> Data {
        guard let space = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB),
              let context = CGContext(data: nil, width: width, height: height, bitsPerComponent: 8, bytesPerRow: width * 4,
                                      space: space, bitmapInfo: CGImageAlphaInfo.premultipliedLast.rawValue) else { return Data() }
        context.setFillColor(red: Double(red) / 255, green: Double(green) / 255, blue: Double(blue) / 255, alpha: Double(alpha) / 255)
        context.fill(CGRect(x: 0, y: 0, width: width, height: height))
        return encode(context.makeImage(), as: .png)
    }

    /// A `width` × `height` sRGB PNG whose every row differs from its neighbours and whose every column does too, opaque; `pixel(_:_:)`
    /// gives the 8-bit colour of the pixel at (x, y), so a test can say what any row must decode to.
    static func colourRows(_ width: Int, _ height: Int) -> Data {
        guard let space = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB),
              let context = CGContext(data: nil, width: width, height: height, bitsPerComponent: 8, bytesPerRow: width * 4,
                                      space: space, bitmapInfo: CGImageAlphaInfo.premultipliedLast.rawValue),
              let memory = context.data else { return Data() }
        let bytes = memory.bindMemory(to: UInt8.self, capacity: width * height * 4)
        for y in 0..<height {
            for x in 0..<width {
                let (red, green, blue) = pixel(x, y)
                let offset = (y * width + x) * 4
                (bytes[offset], bytes[offset + 1], bytes[offset + 2], bytes[offset + 3]) = (UInt8(red), UInt8(green), UInt8(blue), 255)
            }
        }
        return encode(context.makeImage(), as: .png)
    }

    /// The colour `colourRows` paints at (x, y).
    static func pixel(_ x: Int, _ y: Int) -> (Int, Int, Int) { ((x * 6 + y * 5) % 256, (y * 9 + x) % 256, (x * 3 + y * 2) % 256) }

    /// A picture whose pixels look like noise, so its PNG does not shrink to a few bytes and can be cut or damaged mid-stream.
    static func noisy(_ size: Int, type: UTType = .png) -> Data {
        var state: UInt32 = 12_345
        return grey(size, size, type: type) { _, _ in
            state = state &* 1_664_525 &+ 1_013_904_223
            return Int(state >> 24)
        }
    }

    static func encode(_ image: CGImage?, as type: UTType) -> Data {
        guard let image else { return Data() }
        let data = NSMutableData()
        guard let destination = CGImageDestinationCreateWithData(data, type.identifier as CFString, 1, nil) else { return Data() }
        CGImageDestinationAddImage(destination, image, [kCGImageDestinationLossyCompressionQuality: 1.0] as CFDictionary)
        CGImageDestinationFinalize(destination)
        return data as Data
    }

    /// The eight signature bytes and an IHDR chunk claiming `width` × `height`, with nothing after it: what a decompression
    /// bomb's header looks like.
    static func pngHeader(claiming width: UInt32, _ height: UInt32) -> Data {
        var chunk = Data("IHDR".utf8)
        for side in [width, height] { chunk.append(contentsOf: [24, 16, 8, 0].map { side >> UInt32($0) }.map { UInt8($0 & 0xFF) }) }
        chunk.append(contentsOf: [8, 0, 0, 0, 0])
        var file = Data([0x89, 0x50, 0x4E, 0x47, 0x0D, 0x0A, 0x1A, 0x0A, 0, 0, 0, 13])
        file.append(chunk)
        file.append(contentsOf: [24, 16, 8, 0].map { UInt8((crc32(chunk) >> UInt32($0)) & 0xFF) })
        return file
    }

    private static func crc32(_ data: Data) -> UInt32 {
        var crc: UInt32 = 0xFFFF_FFFF
        for byte in data {
            crc ^= UInt32(byte)
            for _ in 0..<8 { crc = crc & 1 == 1 ? (crc >> 1) ^ 0xEDB8_8320 : crc >> 1 }
        }
        return ~crc
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorGraph/Images/ImageProblem.swift` (new file)

```swift
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Why a picture could not be used (fields spec §4, §5), in the plain sentences a node's error and the app's alerts show.
/// Images are untrusted input, so every way a file can be wrong has a case here and none of them is a crash.
public enum ImageProblem: Error, Equatable, Sendable {
    /// An Image Field with no picture chosen.
    case notChosen
    /// More bytes than `ImageLimits.maxBytes`.
    case tooManyBytes(Int)
    /// A side beyond `ImageLimits.maxSide`.
    case tooManyPixels(width: Int, height: Int)
    /// A format ImageIO reads that is not PNG or JPEG.
    case unsupportedType
    /// Not an image at all, or one that is damaged or cut short.
    case undecodable
    /// A linked path that exists but is not a file (a folder, a device, a pipe).
    case notAFile(name: String)
    /// A linked file that exists but could not be read.
    case unreadable(name: String, reason: String)
    /// A linked file that is not where the document says.
    case notFound(name: String, folder: String)
    /// An embedded image whose bytes the document does not hold (a damaged file).
    case embeddedMissing

    public var message: String {
        switch self {
        case .notChosen:
            "Image Field needs an image: choose one in the inspector."
        case .tooManyBytes(let bytes):
            "That image file is \(Int64(bytes).formatted(.byteCount(style: .memory).locale(.messages))); "
                + "images are limited to \(Int64(ImageLimits.maxBytes).formatted(.byteCount(style: .memory).locale(.messages)))."
        case .tooManyPixels(let width, let height):
            "That image is \(width.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))) × \(height.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))) px; "
                + "a side can be at most \(ImageLimits.maxSide.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))) px."
        case .unsupportedType:
            "Only PNG and JPEG images can be used."
        case .undecodable:
            "That file isn't a readable PNG or JPEG image, or it is damaged."
        case .notAFile(let name):
            "Image '\(name)' isn't a file."
        case .unreadable(let name, let reason):
            "Image '\(name)' couldn't be read: \(reason)"
        case .notFound(let name, let folder):
            "Image '\(name)' wasn't found at \(folder). Relink it in the inspector."
        case .embeddedMissing:
            "This embedded image's data is missing from the file, or damaged. Choose the image again in the inspector."
        }
    }
}
```
